DESIGN.md

show
## Concept
The player guides a glowing dot that can temporarily split into two synchronized copies. By timing the split, each copy threads through offset gaps in incoming barriers, then automatically re‑merges to continue forward.

## Player
A single neon dot that drifts forward. The player can move it laterally, tap/click to split the dot into two parallel copies, and let the split automatically re‑merge. The split and merge are the core verbs alongside lateral movement.

## Core loop
The world scrolls toward the dot continuously. Walls with two staggered openings approach. The player moves left or right to align the dot (or its twins) with the openings, optionally tapping to split so each copy can occupy its own gap. After passing the wall the twins drift back together, and the next wall arrives. Each decision—move, split, or stay unified—feeds the moment‑to‑moment tension.

## Hazards / Obstacles
- **Offset‑gap walls**: Solid barriers spanning the screen except for two narrow openings that are horizontally offset. Only a split dot can use both openings simultaneously.
- **Sweeping lasers**: Thin moving beams that sweep vertically or horizontally, requiring precise timing to avoid; they cannot be bypassed by splitting, so they force careful positioning.
- **Rotating spikes**: Obstacles that rotate around a pivot, opening a brief passage that aligns with one gap; the player must split at the right moment to pass safely.

## Score
Score rises each time a wall is successfully cleared. Bonus points are awarded when a split is used to pass through both offset openings on a single wall. Additional points can be earned for narrowly avoiding sweeping lasers.

## Win and lose
The player loses instantly if any part of the dot (single or split) contacts a solid barrier or laser. The player wins after navigating a final "lock" wall that requires a successful split and precise alignment, signaling completion of the challenge. When the game ends, a clear overlay shows "Game Over" or "You Win!" with the final score.

## Feel
A fast‑paced, pulse‑driven rhythm set against a dark backdrop with a single bright dot. Neon glows and crisp synth beeps emphasize each split, merge, and near‑miss, creating an exhilarating blend of reflex and puzzle. The experience feels tight and responsive, rewarding precise timing.

## Why it's fun
The thrill of splitting the dot at just the right instant to slip each half through its own gap delivers a satisfying, almost cinematic moment of precision and control.

TASKS.md

show
1. HTML scaffold + meta.json — create `index.html` with required header, canvas, restart button, and empty script block; generate `meta.json` matching header text.
2. Game state initialization — define player object, split state, hazards array, score, phase, keys set, and constants (canvas size, speeds, split duration, safe radius) at top of script.
3. Main loop implementation — add `startLoop` with `cancelAnimationFrame`, dt calculation, try/catch, and calls to `update(dt)` and `render()`; clear canvas each frame.
4. Input handling — add `keydown`/`keyup` listeners to populate a `keys` Set; add click listener on restart button; implement `reset()` and map `R` key to it.
5. Player movement — implement dt‑scaled lateral movement based on arrow keys/WASD, clamp position within canvas bounds, update visibility flag.
6. Split mechanic — on tap or space key, activate split: create twin positions offset horizontally, start split timer, auto‑merge after duration, adjust rendering and collision checks.
7. Hazard generation — spawn offset‑gap walls and other obstacles respecting `SAFE_RADIUS` and initial safety period; schedule spawns using dt‑scaled timers.
8. Hazard movement — move hazards leftward (world scroll) each frame, removing off‑screen hazards.
9. Collision detection — detect collisions between player (or split halves) and hazards/lasers; on hit transition `phase` to "gameover" and stop active gameplay.
10. Scoring system — increase score when a wall is cleared, award bonus for successful split usage; draw score on canvas top‑left.
11. Rendering — clear canvas, draw background, hazards, player (and split halves), and HUD; ensure player is highest‑contrast element.
12. Game‑over overlay — dim background, display "Game Over" with final score and restart instructions on canvas.
13. Observable contract — populate `window.__game` each frame with `state`, `score`, `player` (x, y, visible), and `objective` description.
14. Audio integration — lazy‑init `AudioContext` in a `beep()` helper; wrap in try/catch and trigger sounds on split, score, and hit events.

    .game-header { padding: 12px 16px; border-bottom: 1px solid #1d1d2a; }
    .game-header h1 { margin: 0; font-size: 18px; color: #7df9ff; }
    .game-header .game-description { margin: 4px 0; color: #a5a5b8; }
    .game-header .game-controls { margin: 4px 0; padding-left: 18px; color: #c8c8d8; }
    main { display: flex; flex-direction: column; align-items: center; padding: 16px; }
    canvas { background: #0f1320; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; }
    .game-restart { margin-top: 10px; padding: 8px 14px; background: #1c2030; color: #7df9ff; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; cursor: pointer; }
    .game-restart:hover { background: #232842; }
  </style>
</head>
<body>
  <header class="game-header">
    <h1>Fragment Split</h1>
    <p class="game-description">Tap to split the dot into two parallel copies that drift apart for a short distance, then automatically re‑merge; use the split to thread through offset gaps that a single dot cannot cross.</p>
    <ul class="game-controls">
      <li>Arrow keys or A/D to move left/right</li>
      <li>Tap or click to split</li>
      <li>Press R or click Restart</li>
    </ul>
  </header>
  <main>
    <canvas id="game" width="800" height="600"></canvas>
    <button id="restart" class="game-restart" type="button">Restart</button>
  </main>
  <script>
    // === CONFIG ==============================================================
    const CANVAS_W = 800;
    const CANVAS_H = 600;
    const PLAYER_SPEED = 500; // px/sec
    const SPLIT_DURATION = 0.5; // seconds
    const SAFE_RADIUS = 200; // px around start, safe for first 3 seconds

    // === STATE ==============================================================
    const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
    const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
    const restartBtn = document.getElementById("restart");

    let player; // { x, y, splitTimer, isSplit }
    let hazards; // array of obstacle objects
    let score;
    let phase; // "playing" | "won" | "lost"
    let keys;
    let lastTime;
    let loopId = null;
    let audioCtx = null;

    function reset() {
      // Initialize player at center bottom
      player = { x: CANVAS_W / 2, y: CANVAS_H - 50, splitTimer: 0, isSplit: false, visible: true };
      hazards = [];
      score = 0;
      phase = "playing";
      keys = new Set();
      lastTime = performance.now();
    }

    // === UPDATE ==============================================================
    function update(dt) {
      if (phase !== "playing") return;

      // Lateral movement based on Arrow keys / WASD.
      const dir = (keys.has("ArrowRight") || keys.has("d") || keys.has("D") ? 1 : 0)
                - (keys.has("ArrowLeft")  || keys.has("a") || keys.has("A") ? 1 : 0);
      player.x += dir * PLAYER_SPEED * dt;

      // Clamp within canvas bounds.
      player.x = Math.max(0, Math.min(CANVAS_W, player.x));

      // Update visibility flag.
      player.visible = player.x >= 0 && player.x <= CANVAS_W && player.y >= 0 && player.y <= CANVAS_H;
    }
  </script>
</body>
</html>

Pre-spawning hazards on top of the player at t=0 has caused frame-1 deaths in past games. Don't do it.\n115: \n116: ### 4. The control actually changes the outcome\n117: \n118: Whatever the player presses must, in the next frame, change a value that affects collision or score. If a control is purely cosmetic (e.g. \"stretch horizontally\" but the gaps are vertical), the game is fundamentally broken even if it runs. Per-frame constants (`x += 5` every frame) make games run 2.4× faster on a 144Hz display.\n123: \n124: ```js\n125: let lastTime = performance.now();\n126: function loop(now) {\n127:   const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1); // seconds, clamped\n128:   lastTime = now;\n129:   // physics scaled by dt:\n130:   player.x += player.vx * dt;\n131:   enemy.y  += enemy.vy  * dt;\n132:   spawnTimer -= dt;\n133:   // ...\n134:   requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n135: }\n136: requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n137: ```\n138: \n139: ### 6. ```
// === CONFIG ==============================================================
const CANVAS_W = 800;
const CANVAS_H = 600;
const PLAYER_SPEED = 500; // px/sec
const SPLIT_DURATION = 0.5; // seconds
const SAFE_RADIUS = 200; // px around start, safe for first 3 seconds

// === STATE ==============================================================
const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
const restartBtn = document.getElementById("restart");

let player; // { x, y, splitTimer, isSplit }
let hazards; // array of obstacle objects
let score;
let phase; // "playing" | "won" | "lost"
let keys;
let lastTime;
let loopId = null;
let audioCtx = null;

function reset() {
  // Initialize player at center bottom
  player = { x: CANVAS_W / 2, y: CANVAS_H - 50, splitTimer: 0, isSplit: false, visible: true };
  hazards = [];
  score = 0;
  phase = "playing";
  keys = new Set();
  lastTime = performance.now();
}

// === UPDATE ==============================================================
function update(dt) {
  if (phase !== "playing") return;

  // Lateral movement based on Arrow keys / WASD.
  const dir = (keys.has("ArrowRight") || keys.has("d") || keys.has("D") ? 1 : 0)
            - (keys.has("ArrowLeft")  || keys.has("a") || keys.has("A") ? 1 : 0);
  player.x += dir * PLAYER_SPEED * dt;

  // Clamp within canvas bounds.
  player.x = Math.max(0, Math.min(CANVAS_W, player.x));

  // Update visibility flag.
  player.visible = player.x >= 0 && player.x <= CANVAS_W && player.y >= 0 && player.y <= CANVAS_H;
}
```

- "canvas appears blank" → the game is loading but `render()` isn't drawing anything. Check that `startLoop()` actually got called at boot, that `render()` runs every frame, and that you're issuing `ctx.fill*` / `ctx.stroke*` calls per entity each frame (not just clearing the canvas).
- "controls list mismatch" → the `<ul class="game-controls">` in `index.html` and the `controls` array in `meta.json` disagree. Pick one source of truth, edit the other to match exactly.
- "playtest: player.visible was false for X% of frames" → the camera/position math is letting the player drift outside `[0, canvas.width) × [0, canvas.height)`. Either clamp the player or update `player.visible` to reflect the truth (don't just hardcode `true`).
- "playtest: state took invalid value" → `window.__game.state` must be exactly `"menu"`, `"playing"`, or `"gameover"`. No other strings.
- "Required file missing: meta.json" → you didn't write the file. Use your write tool now.
- "pageerror" / "console.error" → an exception is being thrown at runtime. Read the message, locate the line, fix the bug.

## Reference example: "breakout"

The following is a complete, validator-passing game. **Use it as a structural template** — your output must follow the same section ordering, the same patterns for the loop / restart / observable contract, and the same overall code style. Do NOT reproduce this game; you are building a different game described in the prompt below. The example exists only to anchor structure.

Notice in particular:
- The `// === SECTION ===` divider banners and their fixed order: CONFIG → STATE → RESET → LOOP/UPDATE → RENDER → INPUT → AUDIO → OBSERVABLE CONTRACT → BOOT.
- Plain object literals for game state (no `class`).
- A `keys` Set polled each frame instead of mutating game state from key handlers.
- `if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` at the top of `startLoop()`.
- The animation loop body wrapped in `try/catch`.
- `publishState()` called once per frame at the end of the tick.
- `window.__game` populated honestly (player.visible reflects actual viewport position).
- AudioContext initialized lazily inside `beep()`, wrapped in try/catch.

<html lang="en">
<head>
  <meta charset="utf-8">
  <title>Neon Breaker</title>
  <style>
    body { margin: 0; background: #0a0a0f; color: #e6e6f0; font-family: ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif; }
    .game-header { padding: 12px 16px; border-bottom: 1px solid #1d1d2a; }
    .game-header h1 { margin: 0; font-size: 18px; color: #7df9ff; }
    .game-header .game-description { margin: 4px 0; color: #a5a5b8; }
    .game-header .game-controls { margin: 4px 0; padding-left: 18px; color: #c8c8d8; }
    main { display: flex; flex-direction: column; align-items: center; padding: 16px; }
    canvas { background: #0f1320; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; }
    .game-restart { margin-top: 10px; padding: 8px 14px; background: #1c2030; color: #7df9ff; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; cursor: pointer; }
    .game-restart:hover { background: #232842; }
  </style>
</head>
<body>
  <header class="game-header">
    <h1>Neon Breaker</h1>
    <p class="game-description">Bounce the ball with the paddle and clear every brick. One ball, no second chances.</p>
    <ul class="game-controls">
      <li>Arrow keys or A/D to move the paddle</li>
      <li>Hold Space to launch faster</li>
      <li>Press R or click Restart</li>
    </ul>
  </header>
  <main>
    <canvas id="game" width="800" height="600"></canvas>
    <button id="restart" class="game-restart" type="button">Restart</button>
  </main>
  <script>
  // === CONFIG =============================================================
  const PADDLE_W = 110;
  const PADDLE_H = 14;
  const PADDLE_Y = 560;
  const PADDLE_SPEED = 700;       // px/sec

  const BALL_R = 7;
  const BALL_BASE_SPEED = 360;    // px/sec at start
  const BALL_MAX_SPEED  = 720;
  const BALL_SPEEDUP_PER_HIT = 6; // px/sec added per brick

  const BRICK_ROWS = 6;
  const BRICK_COLS = 12;
  const BRICK_W = 60;
  const BRICK_H = 20;
  const BRICK_GAP = 4;
  const BRICK_TOP = 60;
  const BRICK_LEFT = (800 - (BRICK_COLS * (BRICK_W + BRICK_GAP) - BRICK_GAP)) / 2;
  const ROW_COLORS = ["#ff52ae", "#ff8c52", "#ffd23f", "#a3ff52", "#52ffae", "#7df9ff"];

  // === STATE ==============================================================
  const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
  const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
  const restartBtn = document.getElementById("restart");

  let paddle;        // { x } center x
  let ball;          // { x, y, vx, vy, speed }
  let bricks;        // array of { x, y, w, h, color, alive }
  let score;
  let bricksLeft;
  let phase;         // "playing" | "won" | "lost"
  let keys;          // pressed key set
  let highScore = 0;
  let lastTime;
  let loopId = null;
  let audioCtx = null;

  function reset() {
    paddle = { x: 400 };
    ball = { x: 400, y: 480, vx: 0, vy: 0, speed: BALL_BASE_SPEED };
    launchBall();
    bricks = [];
    for (let r = 0; r < BRICK_ROWS; r++) {
      for (let c = 0; c < BRICK_COLS; c++) {
        bricks.push({
          x: BRICK_LEFT + c * (BRICK_W + BRICK_GAP),
          y: BRICK_TOP + r * (BRICK_H + BRICK_GAP),
          w: BRICK_W, h: BRICK_H,
          color: ROW_COLORS[r % ROW_COLORS.length],
          alive: true,
        });
      }
    }
    bricksLeft = bricks.length;
    score = 0;
    phase = "playing";
    keys = new Set();
    lastTime = performance.now();
    publishState();
    startLoop();
  }

  function launchBall() {
    // Always launch upward, mild horizontal lean either side.
    const angle = (-Math.PI / 2) + (Math.random() * 0.6 - 0.3);
    ball.vx = Math.cos(angle) * ball.speed;
    ball.vy = Math.sin(angle) * ball.speed;
  }

  function startLoop() {
    if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
    function tick(now) {
      try {
        const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
        lastTime = now;
        update(dt);
        render();
        publishState();
      } catch (err) {
        console.error("loop error", err);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }

  // === UPDATE =============================================================
  function update(dt) {
    if (phase !== "playing") return;

    // Paddle.
    const dirX = (keys.has("ArrowRight") || keys.has("d") || keys.has("D") ? 1 : 0)
               - (keys.has("ArrowLeft")  || keys.has("a") || keys.has("A") ? 1 : 0);
    const speedMul = keys.has(" ") ? 1.6 : 1;
    paddle.x += dirX * PADDLE_SPEED * speedMul * dt;
    paddle.x = clamp(paddle.x, PADDLE_W / 2, canvas.width - PADDLE_W / 2);

    // Ball motion.
    ball.x += ball.vx * dt;
    ball.y += ball.vy * dt;

    // Walls.
    if (ball.x < BALL_R) { ball.x = BALL_R; ball.vx = Math.abs(ball.vx); beep(220, 0.04); }
    if (ball.x > canvas.width - BALL_R) { ball.x = canvas.width - BALL_R; ball.vx = -Math.abs(ball.vx); beep(220, 0.04); }
    if (ball.y < BALL_R) { ball.y = BALL_R; ball.vy = Math.abs(ball.vy); beep(220, 0.04); }

    // Floor — lose.
    if (ball.y > canvas.height + BALL_R) {
      phase = "lost";
      if (score > highScore) highScore = score;
      beep(110, 0.25);
      return;
    }

    // Paddle.
    const px = paddle.x, py = PADDLE_Y;
    if (ball.vy > 0
        && ball.y + BALL_R >= py
        && ball.y - BALL_R <= py + PADDLE_H
        && ball.x >= px - PADDLE_W / 2 - BALL_R
        && ball.x <= px + PADDLE_W / 2 + BALL_R) {
      // Bounce direction depends on where it hit on the paddle.
      const offset = (ball.x - px) / (PADDLE_W / 2); // -1..1
      const angle = (-Math.PI / 2) + offset * 1.0;   // up-ish, leaning side
      ball.vx = Math.cos(angle) * ball.speed;
      ball.vy = Math.sin(angle) * ball.speed;
      ball.y = py - BALL_R - 0.1;
      beep(540, 0.05);
    }

    // Bricks.
    for (const b of bricks) {
      if (!b.alive) continue;
      if (ball.x + BALL_R < b.x || ball.x - BALL_R > b.x + b.w) continue;
      if (ball.y + BALL_R < b.y || ball.y - BALL_R > b.y + b.h) continue;

      b.alive = false;
      bricksLeft--;
      score += 10;
      ball.speed = Math.min(BALL_MAX_SPEED, ball.speed + BALL_SPEEDUP_PER_HIT);

      // Decide which side was hit by comparing penetrations.
      const overlapX = Math.min(ball.x + BALL_R - b.x, b.x + b.w - (ball.x - BALL_R));
      const overlapY = Math.min(ball.y + BALL_R - b.y, b.y + b.h - (ball.y - BALL_R));
      if (overlapX < overlapY) ball.vx = -ball.vx; else ball.vy = -ball.vy;

      // Renormalize to current speed.
      const m = Math.hypot(ball.vx, ball.vy);
      ball.vx = (ball.vx / m) * ball.speed;
      ball.vy = (ball.vy / m) * ball.speed;
      beep(880, 0.05);
      break;
    }

    if (bricksLeft === 0) {
      phase = "won";
      if (score > highScore) highScore = score;
      beep(990, 0.2);
    }
  }

  // === RENDER =============================================================
  function render() {
    ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);

    // Bricks.
    for (const b of bricks) {
      if (!b.alive) continue;
      ctx.fillStyle = b.color;
      ctx.fillRect(b.x, b.y, b.w, b.h);
    }

    // Paddle.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
    ctx.fillRect(paddle.x - PADDLE_W / 2, PADDLE_Y, PADDLE_W, PADDLE_H);

    // Ball.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#ffffff";
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.arc(ball.x, ball.y, BALL_R, 0, Math.PI * 2);
    ctx.fill();

    // HUD.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
    ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
    ctx.textAlign = "left";
    ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
    ctx.textAlign = "right";
    ctx.fillText(`Bricks ${bricksLeft}  ·  Best ${highScore}`, canvas.width - 12, 22);

    if (phase === "lost" || phase === "won") {
      ctx.fillStyle = "rgba(10,10,15,0.7)";
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
      ctx.textAlign = "center";
      ctx.font = "44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillStyle = phase === "won" ? "#52ffae" : "#ff52ae";
      ctx.fillText(phase === "won" ? "You Win!" : "Game Over", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 - 20);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
      ctx.font = "20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}`, canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 14);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#a5a5b8";
      ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText("Press R or click Restart", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 42);
    }
  }

  // === INPUT ==============================================================
  document.addEventListener("keydown", (ev) => {
    if (ev.key === "r" || ev.key === "R") { reset(); return; }
    keys.add(ev.key);
  });
  document.addEventListener("keyup", (ev) => { keys.delete(ev.key); });
  restartBtn.addEventListener("click", reset);

  // === AUDIO (lazy) =======================================================
  function beep(freq, durSec) {
    try {
      if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
      const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
      const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
      osc.type = "square";
      osc.frequency.value = freq;
      gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.05, audioCtx.currentTime);
      gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
      osc.connect(gain); gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
      osc.start(); osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    } catch {}
  }

  // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ===============================================
  function publishState() {
    window.__game = {
      state: phase === "playing" ? "playing" : "gameover",
      score,
      player: {
        x: paddle.x,
        y: PADDLE_Y + PADDLE_H / 2,
        visible: paddle.x >= 0 && paddle.x <= canvas.width,
      },
      objective: "Bounce the ball with the paddle to break every brick.",
    };
  }

  // === HELPERS ============================================================
  function clamp(v, lo, hi) { return v < lo ? lo : (v > hi ? hi : v); }

  // === BOOT ===============================================================
  reset();
  </script>
</body>
</html>
```

Title: Fragment Split
Concept: Tap to split the dot into two parallel copies that drift apart for a short distance, then automatically re‑merge; use the split to thread through offset gaps that a single dot cannot cross.

Use the reference example above as your structural template — section divider banners, the `keys` Set input pattern, the `cancelAnimationFrame` discipline at the top of `startLoop()`, the lazy AudioContext, the `publishState()` call once per frame.

Clicking it must fully reset the game (same code path as the R key). It must be visible and clickable at all times — not only on game-over.\n72: - The `<header>`'s `<h1>` text must match `meta.json.title` exactly.\n73: - The `<p class=\"game-description\">` text must match `meta.json.description` exactly.\n74: - The `<li>` items in `<ul class=\"game-controls\">` must match `meta.json.controls` in order.\n75: \n76: ### Behavior\n77: - The game must start rendering immediately on page load (no \"click to start\" required).\n78: - Use keyboard and/or mouse input.\n79: - The script must not throw any uncaught exception in the first 5 seconds.\n80: - If you use `localStorage` or `sessionStorage`, all keys must start with the prefix `slop-factory:fragment-split:`.\n81: \n82: ### Style\n83: - Dark background (the gallery is dark — recommend `#0a0a0f` or similar).\n84: - High-contrast neon accents (e.g. cyan `#7df9ff`, magenta `#ff00aa`, yellow `#ffd23f`).\n85: - The `<header>` is shown above the canvas — keep its styling minimal and let the canvas content shine.\n86: \n87: ## What makes a tiny canvas game feel good\n88: \n89: Aim for these qualities in every game you produce:\n90: \n91: 1. **Immediate input response** — every keystroke or click produces visible action within one frame. Never make the player wait.\n92: 2. **Clear feedback** — the player always knows when something happened. Use color flashes, particles, brief screen shake, score popups, or sound on every meaningful event (hit, score, miss, death).\n93: 3. **Win/lose state** — the game has a clear end condition. On lose, draw \"Game Over\" + final score + restart instructions on the canvas. On win, celebrate (color burst, \"You Win!\" overlay).\n94: 4. **Restart without page reload** — both pressing R AND clicking the `<button id=\"restart\">` reset the game cleanly via the same code path. Mention both in `controls` (e.g. \"Press R or click Restart\"). On restart you MUST `cancelAnimationFrame` any pending loop before starting a new one (otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game eventually slows to a crawl).\n95: 5. **Progressive difficulty** — start gentle so the player can learn, then ramp up. Don't dump them into chaos at t=0.\n96: 6. **No dead time** — something interesting on screen at all times. Idle background animation (stars, gradient pulse, particles) is fine.\n97: 7. **Persistent score / progress UI** — drawn on the canvas at the top corner, always visible.\n98: 8. **Audio (optional but encouraged)** — generate tones with `OscillatorNode` (no external assets). **Do not auto-start audio on page load** — browsers block this and it will throw. Initialize the `AudioContext` lazily on the first keypress or click. Wrap audio code in try/catch.\n99: \n100: ## Playable Contract — non-negotiable\n101: \n102: These are not aspirations. A game that violates any of these is broken even if it runs without errors. After your implementation finishes, the validator loads the page in a real browser and runs an 8-second playtest with synthetic input — failures here are reported back to you and must be fixed before the game ships.\n103: \n104: ### 1. The player can lose\n105: \n106: There must be a collision check (or other end condition) that fires `gameOver` and stops the active gameplay loop. \"Game runs forever, no death\" is broken. Examples that have failed in the past: collision check compared a value against itself; \"spike\" collision only clamped position instead of killing the player; obstacles never reached the player.\n107: \n108: ### 2. The player can score\n109: \n110: The score must increase on a **discrete event** (a survived obstacle, a collected pickup, an enemy destroyed, a beat hit). \"Score = elapsed seconds\" is allowed but weak. Score must be drawn on the canvas at all times.\n111: \n112: ### 3. The first 3 seconds are survivable with no input\n113: \n114: Define a `SAFE_RADIUS` (e.g. 200px) around the player's starting position. When spawning hazards, **reject any spawn that is within `SAFE_RADIUS` of the player AND would reach them within 3 seconds at current hazard speed**. Pre-spawning hazards on top of the player at t=0 has caused frame-1 deaths in past games. Don't do it.\n115: \n116: ### 4. The control actually changes the outcome\n117: \n118: Whatever the player presses must, in the next frame, change a value that affects collision or score. If a control is purely cosmetic (e.g. \"stretch horizontally\" but the gaps are vertical), the game is fundamentally broken even if it runs. After writing, **re-read the game's concept and trace one frame**: does the input do what the concept claims?\n119: \n120: ### 5. dt-based physics, not per-frame constants\n121: \n122: All movement and timing must scale by delta time (seconds since last frame). Per-frame constants (`x += 5` every frame) make games run 2.4× faster on a 144Hz display.\n123: \n124: ```js\n125: let lastTime = performance.now();\n126: function loop(now) {\n127:   const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1); // seconds, clamped\n128:   lastTime = now;\n129:   // physics scaled by dt:\n130:   player.x += player.vx * dt;\n131:   enemy.y  += enemy.vy  * dt;\n132:   spawnTimer -= dt;\n133:   // ...\n134:   requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n135: }\n136: requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n137: ```\n138: \n139: ### 6. Restart fully resets AND cancels the prior loop\n140: \n141: Both R and the Restart button must call the same reset function. That function MUST `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` the previous loop before scheduling a new one — otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game slows to a crawl over time.\n142: \n143: ```js\n144: let loopId = null;\n145: function startLoop() {\n146:   if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);\n147:   function tick(now) { /* ... */ loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick); }\n148:   loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);\n149: }\n150: ```\n151: \n152: ### 7. Game-over overlay shows score and restart instructions\n153: \n154: When the player dies, draw on the canvas: \"Game Over\", final score, current high score (if you persist one), and \"Press R or click Restart\". Do not rely on the HTML restart button alone — players need to know they can restart.\n155: \n156: ## Observable State Contract — required for playtest\n157: \n158: In addition to the rules above, your game **must** expose its current state on `window.__game` so the automated playtest can observe what the player would observe. A game that does not expose this object is rejected.\n159: \n160: ```js\n161: window.__game = {\n162:   state: \"menu\",        // \"menu\" | \"playing\" | \"gameover\" — exact strings, no others\n163:   score: 0,             // current score, number\n164:   player: {\n165:     x: 400,             // player x in canvas coordinates (0 = left)\n166:     y: 300,             // player y in canvas coordinates (0 = top)\n167:     visible: true,      // true if the player is currently rendered inside the canvas viewport\n168:   },\n169:   objective: \"Dodge falling neon blocks. Survive as long as possible.\", // 10-200 chars, one sentence\n170: };\n171: ```\n172: \n173: Rules:\n174: - The object must exist from the very first frame, before any input.\n175: - Update `state`, `score`, `player.x`, `player.y`, and `player.visible` whenever they change. The simplest approach is to update them at the end of your render/update step each frame.\n176: - `state` must only ever be one of the three exact strings. No other values.\n177: - `player.visible` is critical. **If your camera follows the player, the camera math must keep `player.visible` = true at all times.** A camera that lags the player off-screen is the most common failure here.\n178: - `objective` is one short sentence written for a first-time player: \"what am I trying to do?\" — derivable from observing the game alone, not from reading the meta.\n179: \n180: If your game has multiple \"players\" or no clear single player, expose the position of the most player-like entity (the thing the human controls).\n181: \n182: ## What the validator checks\n183: \n184: Your output is checked programmatically. No external URLs in `<script src=>`, `<link href=>`, `<img src=>` (regex scan).\n190: 5. Headless Chromium loads the page; no `console.error` or `pageerror` events in 5 seconds.\n191: 6. Canvas has non-blank pixels after 3 seconds (game is actually drawing something).\n192: 7. **Observable contract is present**: `window.__game` exists with the exact shape above, from the first frame.\n193: 8. **Playtest invariants** hold during ~8 seconds of synthetic key input (arrow keys, WASD, space):\n194:    - `state` only takes the values `\"menu\" | \"playing\" | \"gameover\"`.\n195:    - `player.visible` is true for ≥95% of polled frames.\n196:    - Either `score` changes at least once during play, or `state` reaches `\"gameover\"`.     function reset() {

function startLoop() {

Let's implement startLoop:

```
function startLoop() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  function tick(now) {
    try {
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;
      // clear canvas
      ctx.clearRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
      update(dt);
      render();
    } catch (err) {
      console.error("loop error", err);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}
```

// === UPDATE ==============================================================
function update(dt) {
  if (phase !== "playing") return;
  // Player movement
  const dir = (keys.has("ArrowRight") || keys.has("d") || keys.has("D") ? 1 : 0)
            - (keys.has("ArrowLeft") || keys.has("a") || keys.has("A") ? 1 : 0);
  player.x += dir * PLAYER_SPEED * dt;
  // Clamp within canvas
  const halfW = 0; // dot has no width; we can clamp to 0..CANVAS_W
  player.x = Math.max(0, Math.min(CANVAS_W, player.x));
  // visibility
  player.visible = player.x >= 0 && player.x <= CANVAS_W && player.y >= 0 && player.y <= CANVAS_H;
}
```

function startLoop() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  function tick(now) {
    try {
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;
      update(dt);
      render();
    } catch (err) {
      console.error('loop error', err);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}

Add updateHazards code:

function updateHazards(dt) {
   const speed = WALL_SPEED;
   for (const h of hazards) {
       h.y += speed * dt;
   }
   // remove hazards that passed beyond canvas
   hazards = hazards.filter(h => h.y < CANVAS_H + SAFE_RADIUS);
}

Title: Fragment Split
Concept: Tap to split the dot into two parallel copies that drift apart for a short distance, then automatically re‑merge; use the split to thread through offset gaps that a single dot cannot cross.

**You must produce one output file: `TASKS.md` in the working directory.** Use your write/edit tool to create this file on disk. After your turn ends, the validator runs `readFile("TASKS.md")` — if the file does not exist on disk, validation fails and your work is wasted. **Do NOT reply with the task list as inline chat text.** The list must be written to the file.

**Tool calls MUST be emitted as actual function/tool invocations — NOT described or simulated inside your reasoning/analysis text.** Writing tool-shaped JSON like `{"filePath":"TASKS.md","content":"..."}` *inside your chain-of-thought* does NOT invoke any tool. It is just text in the analysis channel that nothing reads or executes. The same goes for `{"pattern":"**/*"}` etc. — those only do something when emitted as a real `tool_use` event from the action channel, never when written into your thinking. Several prior runs have failed by hallucinating tool calls inside reasoning; do not repeat that pattern. If at any point you "describe" a tool call in your thinking instead of invoking it, you must immediately invoke the real tool afterward. The format MUST be a numbered list, one task per line, each line starting with `<number>.`:

```
1. <Task title> — <one-sentence description of what to do>
2. <Task title> — <one-sentence description>
...
```

The next stage parses tasks by matching `^\d+\.` at the start of each line. Don't use bullet sub-lists, don't number sub-tasks (1.1, 1.2). One number, one task, one line.

### What makes a good task

- **Small enough** to complete in a single coding turn (~50–150 lines of edits).
- **Testable**: after this task, what does the page do that it didn't before?
- **Leaves the file runnable**: at the end of the task, no syntax errors, no uncaught exceptions on load. Even if the game isn't playable yet, it must load.
- **Specific**: don't say "implement player". Say "implement player movement: arrow keys set keys.left/right; in update(), apply acceleration in the pressed direction with cap at MAX_SPEED, friction otherwise; clamp x to canvas bounds."
- **Concrete numbers**: this is where pixel sizes, ms intervals, and px/sec speeds live. Pick them. The implement stage will use them verbatim.

### Required coverage (in roughly this order)

Cover at minimum, ordered as below. Add tasks if the design demands more (powerups, level progression, screen wrap, etc):

1. **HTML scaffold + meta.json**: header (h1, description, controls list), canvas, restart button per CONVENTIONS.md. Stub `<script>` block (empty). meta.json matching header text.
2. **Game state objects**: player, hazards, score, phase using plain object literals at the top of the script. Set initial values.
3. **requestAnimationFrame loop**: startLoop() with `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` discipline at the top. Body wrapped in try/catch. Calls update(dt) and render(). Don't draw anything yet besides clearing the canvas.
4. **Input**: a `keys` Set populated by document keydown/keyup. R-key restart hook. Restart button click hook. Both call the same reset() function.
5. **Player movement**: dt-based physics tied to the key inputs from task 4. Verify each control listed in meta.json actually changes a value.
6. **Hazards**: data structure, spawn rules with a SAFE_RADIUS so nothing spawns on top of the player at t=0, motion (dt-scaled).
7. **Collision detection**: player vs hazards. On hit, transition phase to "gameover" and stop the active gameplay.
8. **Score**: discrete events that increase score, drawn on canvas at top-left.
9. **Render**: clear canvas, draw background, hazards, player, HUD. Player must be the highest-contrast object on screen.
10. **Game-over overlay**: dim background, "Game Over", final score, "Press R or click Restart".
11. **Observable contract**: populate `window.__game = { state, score, player:{x,y,visible}, objective }` every frame. `state` is "playing" | "gameover". `objective` is a 10–200 char single-sentence description.
12. **Audio**: lazy-init AudioContext inside a beep() helper, wrapped in try/catch. Hook into hit / score / death events.

You may merge two trivial tasks if they truly belong together (e.g. tasks 1+2 if state is just three lines). You may split a complex task in two. Aim for 10–15 tasks total.

**Final check before you stop**: confirm you actually called your write/edit tool with `TASKS.md` as the path.
